== Conventions

Version requirements are specified as conditions using the following operators:

[cols="1,2"]
|===
| Operator | Meaning

| `~> VERSION` | Accepts any version that is _compatible_ with `VERSION`. By default, a version A is compatible with a version B if A's version number is greater than or equal to version B. If that default assumption is ever broken, it will be noted in the list of extension versions.
| `= VERSION` | Accepts only version `VERSION`.
| `>= VERSION` | Accepts any version greater than or equal to `VERSION`.
| `\<= VERSION` | Accepts any version less than or equal to `VERSION`.
| `> VERSION` | Accepts any version greater than `VERSION`.
| `< VERSION` | Accepts any version less than `VERSION`.
|===

Control and status register instructions

== CSR summary


<<<
[#csrs,reftext="CSRs (in alphabetical order)"]
== Csrs (in alphabetical order)

<<<
:leveloffset: +2

[#udb:doc:csr:fcsr]
= fcsr

*Floating-point control and status register (`frm` + `fflags`)*

The floating-point control and status register, `fcsr`, is a RISC-V
control and status register (CSR). It is a 32-bit read/write register
that selects the dynamic rounding mode for floating-point arithmetic
operations and holds the accrued exception flags, as shown in <<fcsr>>.

[[fcsr, Floating-Point Control and Status Register]]
.Floating-point control and status register
include::images/wavedrom/float-csr.adoc[]

The `fcsr` register can be read and written with the FRCSR and FSCSR
instructions, which are assembler pseudoinstructions built on the
underlying CSR access instructions. FRCSR reads `fcsr` by copying it
into integer register _rd_. FSCSR swaps the value in `fcsr` by copying
the original value into integer register _rd_, and then writing a new
value obtained from integer register _rs1_ into `fcsr`.

The fields within the `fcsr` can also be accessed individually through
different CSR addresses, and separate assembler pseudoinstructions are defined
for these accesses. The FRRM instruction reads the Rounding Mode field `frm`
(`fcsr` bits 7--5) and copies it into the least-significant three bits of
integer register _rd_, with zero in all other bits. FSRM swaps the value in
`frm` by copying the original value into integer register _rd_, and then
writing a new value obtained from the three least-significant bits of integer
register _rs1_ into `frm`. FRFLAGS and FSFLAGS are defined analogously for the
Accrued Exception Flags field `fflags` (`fcsr` bits 4--0).

Bits 31--8 of the `fcsr` are reserved for other standard extensions. If
these extensions are not present, implementations shall ignore writes to
these bits and supply a zero value when read. Standard software should
preserve the contents of these bits.

Floating-point operations use either a static rounding mode encoded in
the instruction, or a dynamic rounding mode held in `frm`. Rounding
modes are encoded as shown in <<rm>>. A value of 111 in the
instruction's _rm_ field selects the dynamic rounding mode held in
`frm`. The behavior of floating-point instructions that depend on
rounding mode when executed with a reserved rounding mode is _reserved_, including both static reserved rounding modes (101-110) and dynamic reserved rounding modes (101-111). Some instructions, including widening conversions, have the _rm_ field but are nevertheless mathematically unaffected by the rounding mode; software should set their _rm_ field to
RNE (000) but implementations must treat the _rm_ field as usual (in
particular, with regard to decoding legal vs. reserved encodings).

[NOTE]
====
The C99 language standard effectively mandates the provision of a
dynamic rounding mode register. In typical implementations, writes to
the dynamic rounding mode CSR state will serialize the pipeline. Static
rounding modes are used to implement specialized arithmetic operations
that often have to switch frequently between different rounding modes.

The ratified version of the F spec mandated that an illegal-instruction
exception was raised when an instruction was executed with a reserved
dynamic rounding mode. This has been weakened to reserved, which matches
the behavior of static rounding-mode instructions. Raising an
illegal-instruction exception is still valid behavior when encountering a
reserved encoding, so implementations compatible with the ratified spec
are compatible with the weakened spec.
====

The accrued exception flags indicate the exception conditions that have
arisen on any floating-point arithmetic instruction since the field was
last reset by software, as shown in <<bitdef>>. The base
RISC-V ISA does not support generating a trap on the setting of a
floating-point exception flag.
(((floating-point, exception flag)))

[[bitdef]]
.Accrued exception flag encoding.
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="^,<",options="header",]
|===
|Flag Mnemonic |Flag Meaning
|NV |Invalid Operation
|DZ |Divide by Zero
|OF |Overflow
|UF |Underflow
|NX |Inexact
|===

[NOTE]
====
As allowed by the standard, we do not support traps on floating-point
exceptions in the F extension, but instead require explicit checks of
the flags in software. We considered adding branches controlled directly
by the contents of the floating-point accrued exception flags, but
ultimately chose to omit these instructions to keep the ISA simple.
====

!===

....

Rounding modes are encoded as follows:

[[rm]]
.Rounding mode encoding.
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="^,^,<",options="header"]
!===
!Rounding Mode |Mnemonic |Meaning
!000 !RNE !Round to Nearest, ties to Even
!001 !RTZ !Round towards Zero
!010 !RDN !Round Down (towards latexmath:[$-\infty$])
!011 !RUP !Round Up (towards latexmath:[$+\infty$])
!100 !RMM !Round to Nearest, ties to Max Magnitude
!101 ! !_Reserved for future use._
!110 ! !_Reserved for future use._
!111 !DYN !In instruction's _rm_ field, selects dynamic rounding mode; In Rounding Mode register, _reserved_.
!===

A value of 111 in the
instruction's _rm_ field selects the dynamic rounding mode held in
`frm`. The behavior of floating-point instructions that depend on
rounding mode when executed with a reserved rounding mode is _reserved_,
including both static reserved rounding modes (101-110) and dynamic reserved
rounding modes (101-111). Some instructions, including widening conversions,
have the _rm_ field but are nevertheless mathematically unaffected by the
rounding mode; software should set their _rm_ field to
RNE (000) but implementations must treat the _rm_ field as usual (in
particular, with regard to decoding legal vs. reserved encodings).

[#udb:doc:csr:fflags]
= fflags

*Floating-Point Accrued Exceptions*

The accrued exception flags indicate the exception conditions that have arisen on any floating-point arithmetic
instruction since the field was last reset by software.

The base RISC-V ISA does not support generating a trap on the setting of a floating-point exception flag.

As allowed by the standard, we do not support traps on floating-point exceptions in the F
extension, but instead require explicit checks of the flags in software. We considered
adding branches controlled directly by the contents of the floating-point accrued
exception flags, but ultimately chose to omit these instructions to keep the ISA simple.

== Software write

This CSR may store a value that is different from what software attempts to write.

When a software write occurs (_e.g._, through xref:#udb-insn-csrrw[`csrrw`]), the following determines the
written value:

[source,idl]
----
NV = CSR[fcsr].NV = csr_value.NV;
return csr_value.NV;

DZ = CSR[fcsr].DZ = csr_value.DZ;
return csr_value.DZ;

OF = CSR[fcsr].OF = csr_value.OF;
return csr_value.OF;

UF = CSR[fcsr].UF = csr_value.UF;
return csr_value.UF;

NX = CSR[fcsr].NX = csr_value.NX;
return csr_value.NX;

----

== Software read

This CSR may return a value that is different from what is stored in hardware.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
return (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-fcsr[fcsr]].xref:#udb-csrfield-fcsr-NV[NV] pass:[`]<< 4) | (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-fcsr[fcsr]].xref:#udb-csrfield-fcsr-DZ[DZ] pass:[`]<< 3) | (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-fcsr[fcsr]].xref:#udb-csrfield-fcsr-OF[OF] pass:[`]<< 2) | (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-fcsr[fcsr]].xref:#udb-csrfield-fcsr-UF[UF] pass:[`]<< 1) | CSR[xref:#udb-csr-fcsr[fcsr]].xref:#udb-csrfield-fcsr-NX[NX];
----

[#udb:doc:csr:frm]
= frm

*Floating-Point Dynamic Rounding Mode*

Rounding modes are encoded as follows:

[[rm]]
.Rounding mode encoding.
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="^,^,<",options="header"]
!===
!Rounding Mode |Mnemonic |Meaning
!000 !RNE !Round to Nearest, ties to Even
!001 !RTZ !Round towards Zero
!010 !RDN !Round Down (towards latexmath:[$-\infty$])
!011 !RUP !Round Up (towards latexmath:[$+\infty$])
!100 !RMM !Round to Nearest, ties to Max Magnitude
!101 ! !_Reserved for future use._
!110 ! !_Reserved for future use._
!111 !DYN !In instruction's _rm_ field, selects dynamic rounding mode; In Rounding Mode register, _reserved_.
!===

The behavior of floating-point instructions that depend on rounding mode when
executed with a reserved rounding mode is _reserved_, including both static
reserved rounding modes (101-110) and dynamic reserved rounding modes (101-111).

Some instructions, including widening conversions, have the _rm_ field but are
nevertheless mathematically unaffected by the rounding mode; software should set
their _rm_ field to RNE (000) but implementations must treat the _rm_ field as
usual (in particular, with regard to decoding legal vs. reserved encodings).

h^ Defining extensions
a^

!===

!===

h^ Length         ^ 32-bit
h^ Privilege Mode ^ U
|===

[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":3,"name":"ROUNDINGMODE","type":3},{"bits":29,"type":1}],"config":{"bits":32,"lanes":2}}
....

@ UNDEFINED_LEGAL

== Software read

This CSR may return a value that is different from what is stored in hardware.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::S) {
  if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::U) {
  if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-misa[misa]].xref:#udb-csrfield-misa-S[S] == 1'b1) {
    if pass:[(](CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] & CSR[xref:#udb-csr-scounteren[scounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-scounteren-HPM10[HPM10]) == 1'b0) {
      xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
    }
  } else if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::VS) {
  if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-hcounteren[hcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-hcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b0 && CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b1) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::VirtualInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  } else if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::VU) {
  if pass:[(]((CSR[xref:#udb-csr-hcounteren[hcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-hcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] & CSR[xref:#udb-csr-scounteren[scounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-scounteren-HPM10[HPM10]) == 1'b0) && (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b1)) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::VirtualInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  } else if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-HPM10[HPM10] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
}
return xref:#udb-function-read_hpm_counter[read_hpm_counter]pass:[(]10);
----

= cycle

*Cycle counter for RDCYCLE Instruction*

Alias for M-mode CSR xref:#udb-csr-mcycle[`mcycle`].

Privilege mode access is controlled with `mcounteren.CY`, `scounteren.CY`, and `hcounteren.CY` as follows:

!===
.2+h![.rotate]#`mcounteren.CY`# .2+h! [.rotate]#`scounteren.CY`# .2+h! [.rotate]#`hcounteren.CY`#
4+^.>h! xref:#udb-csr-cycle[`cycle`] behavior
.^h! S-mode .^h! U-mode .^h! VS-mode .^h! VU-mode

! 0 ! - ! - ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `IllegalInstruction`
! 1 ! 0 ! 0 ! read-only ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `VirtualInstruction` ! `VirtualInstruction`
! 1 ! 1 ! 0 ! read-only ! read-only ! `VirtualInstruction` ! `VirtualInstruction`
! 1 ! 0 ! 1 ! read-only ! `IllegalInstruction` ! read-only ! `VirtualInstruction`
! 1 ! 1 ! 1 ! read-only ! read-only ! read-only ! read-only

! 0 ! - ! - ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `IllegalInstruction`
! 1 ! 0 ! 0 ! read-only ! `IllegalInstruction` ! `VirtualInstruction` ! `VirtualInstruction`
! 1 ! 1 ! 0 ! read-only ! read-only ! `VirtualInstruction` ! `VirtualInstruction`

[#udb:doc:csr:jvt]
= jvt

*Table Jump Base Vector and Control Register*

The `jvt` register is an XLEN-bit WARL read/write register that holds the jump table configuration,
consisting of the jump table base address (BASE) and the jump table mode (MODE).

`jvt` CSR adds architectural state to the system software context (such as an OS process), therefore
must be saved/restored on context switches.


== Attributes
[%autowidth,separator="^"]
|===
h^ Requirement a^ `Zcmt`
h^ Defining extensions
a^

!===

h! Zcmt ! Table Jump
!===

h^ CSR Address    ^ 0x17
h^ Length         ^ -bit
h^ Privilege Mode ^ U
|===

== Format
.jvt format
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":6,"name":"MODE","type":3},{"bits":58,"name":"BASE","type":3}],"config":{"bits":64,"lanes":4}}
....

== Field Summary

[%autowidth,separator=@,float="center",align="center",cols="^,<,<,<",options="header",role="stretch"]
|===
@Name @ Location @ Type @ Reset Value

@ xref:#udb-csrfield-jvt-BASE[jvt.BASE]
@ 31:6
@ [when,"JVT_READ_ONLY"]
RO
[when,"!(JVT_READ_ONLY)"]
RW

@ UNDEFINED_LEGAL

@ xref:#udb-csrfield-jvt-MODE[jvt.MODE]
@ 5:0
@ [when,"JVT_READ_ONLY"]
RO
[when,"!(JVT_READ_ONLY)"]
RW

@ 0

|===


== Fields


[#udb:doc:csr_field:jvt:BASE]
=== `BASE`

[example]
--
Location::
31:6

Description::
The value in the BASE field must always be aligned on a 64-byte boundary. Note that the CSR contains only
bits XLEN-1 through 6 of the address base. When computing jump-table accesses, the lower six bits of base
are filled with zeroes to obtain an XLEN-bit jump-table base address `jvt.base` that is always aligned on a
64-byte boundary.

`jvt.base` is a virtual address, whenever virtual memory is enabled.

The memory pointed to by `jvt.base` is treated as instruction memory for the purpose of executing table
jump instructions, implying execute access permission.


Type::
[when,"JVT_READ_ONLY"]
RO
[when,"!(JVT_READ_ONLY)"]
RW


Reset value::
UNDEFINED_LEGAL

--

[#udb:doc:csr_field:jvt:MODE]
=== `MODE`

[example]
--
Location::
5:0

Description::
`jvt.mode` is a WARL field, so can only be programmed to modes which are implemented. Therefore the
discovery mechanism is to attempt to program different modes and read back the values to see which
are available. Jump table mode must be implemented.


Type::
[when,"JVT_READ_ONLY"]
RO
[when,"!(JVT_READ_ONLY)"]
RW


Reset value::
0

--


== Software write

This CSR may store a value that is different from what software attempts to write.

When a software write occurs (_e.g._, through xref:#udb-insn-csrrw[`csrrw`]), the following determines the
written value:

[source,idl]
----
BASE = if (( mode() != PrivilegeMode::M && implemented?(ExtensionName::Smstateen)
      && CSR[mstateen0].JVT == 1'b0 )
    ||
    ( mode() == PrivilegeMode::U && implemented?(ExtensionName::Ssstateen)
      && CSR[sstateen0].JVT == 1'b0 )
    ||
    ( mode() == PrivilegeMode::VS && implemented?(ExtensionName::Ssstateen)
      && CSR[hstateen0].JVT == 1'b0 )
    ||
    ( mode() == PrivilegeMode::VU && implemented?(ExtensionName::Ssstateen)
      && (CSR[sstateen0].JVT == 1'b0 || CSR[hstateen0].JVT == 1'b0) )) {

  unimplemented_csr($encoding);
  return 0;
}
else {
  if (JVT_BASE_TYPE == "custom") {
    unpredictable("jvt.BASE has custom behavior");
  } else {
    return csr_value.BASE & (JVT_BASE_MASK >> 6);
  }
  return csr_value.BASE == 0 ? 0 : UNDEFINED_LEGAL_DETERMINISTIC;
}

MODE = if (JVT_READ_ONLY || (csr_value.MODE != 0)) {
  raise(ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, mode(), $encoding);
}
return 0;

----

== Software read

This CSR may return a value that is different from what is stored in hardware.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_zcmt_enabled[check_zcmt_enabled]pass:[(]$encoding);
return $bits(CSR[xref:#udb-csr-jvt[CSR[jvt]]]);
----

:leveloffset: -2

[#udb:doc:csr:ssp]
= ssp

*Shadow Stack Pointer*

The `ssp` CSR is an unprivileged read-write (URW) CSR that reads and writes
XLEN low order bits of the shadow stack pointer. The `ssp` is always as wide
as the XLEN of the current privilege mode. The bits 1:0 of ssp are read-only
zero. If the UXLEN or SXLEN may never be 32, then the bit 2 is also read-only
zero.


== Attributes
[%autowidth,separator="^"]
|===
h^ Requirement a^ `Zicfiss`
h^ Defining extensions
a^

!===

h! Zicfiss ! Shadow Stack
!===

h^ CSR Address    ^ 0x11
h^ Length         ^ -bit
h^ Privilege Mode ^ U
|===

== Format
.ssp format
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":3,"type":1},{"bits":61,"name":"VALUE","type":3}],"config":{"bits":64,"lanes":4}}
....

== Field Summary

[%autowidth,separator=@,float="center",align="center",cols="^,<,<,<",options="header",role="stretch"]
|===
@Name @ Location @ Type @ Reset Value

@ xref:#udb-csrfield-ssp-VALUE[ssp.VALUE]
@ 31:2
@ RW
@ UNDEFINED_LEGAL

|===


== Fields


[#udb:doc:csr_field:ssp:VALUE]
=== `VALUE`

[example]
--
Location::
31:2

Description::
The value in ssp points to the top of the shadow stack, which is the address
of the last element stored on the shadow stack.


Type::
RW

Reset value::
UNDEFINED_LEGAL

--


== Software write

This CSR may store a value that is different from what software attempts to write.

When a software write occurs (_e.g._, through xref:#udb-insn-csrrw[`csrrw`]), the following determines the
written value:

[source,idl]
----
VALUE = if ((mode() < PrivilegeMode::M && CSR[menvcfg].SSE == 0) ||
    (mode() == PrivilegeMode::U && CSR[senvcfg].SSE == 0)) {
  raise(ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, mode(), $encoding);
} else if ((mode() == PrivilegeMode::VS && CSR[henvcfg].SSE == 0) ||
           (mode() == PrivilegeMode::VU && (CSR[henvcfg].SSE == 0 || CSR[senvcfg].SSE == 0))) {
  raise(ExceptionCode::VirtualInstruction, mode(), $encoding);
} else {
  return csr_value.VALUE;
}

----

== Software read

This CSR may return a value that is different from what is stored in hardware.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
if pass:[(](xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) < PrivilegeMode::M && CSR[xref:#udb-csr-menvcfg[menvcfg]].xref:#udb-csrfield-menvcfg-SSE[SSE] == 0) || (xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::U && CSR[xref:#udb-csr-senvcfg[senvcfg]].xref:#udb-csrfield-senvcfg-SSE[SSE] == 0)) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(](xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::VS && CSR[xref:#udb-csr-henvcfg[henvcfg]].xref:#udb-csrfield-henvcfg-SSE[SSE] == 0) || (xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::VU && (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-henvcfg[henvcfg]].xref:#udb-csrfield-henvcfg-SSE[SSE] == 0 || CSR[xref:#udb-csr-senvcfg[senvcfg]].xref:#udb-csrfield-senvcfg-SSE[SSE] == 0))) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::VirtualInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-xlen[xlen]pass:[(]) == 32) {
  return ($bits(CSR[xref:#udb-csr-ssp[CSR[ssp]]]) & ~MXLEN'3);
}
return ($bits(CSR[xref:#udb-csr-ssp[CSR[ssp]]]) & ~MXLEN'7);
----

[#udb:doc:csr:time]
= time

*Timer for RDTIME Instruction*

[when,"TIME_CSR_IMPLEMENTED == false"]
This CSR does not exist, and access will cause an IllegalInstruction exception.

[when,"TIME_CSR_IMPLEMENTED == true"]
--
Shadow of the memory-mapped M-mode CSR `mtime`.

Privilege mode access is controlled with `mcounteren.TM`, `scounteren.TM`, and `hcounteren.TM` as follows:

[%autowidth,cols="1,1,1,1,1,1,1",separator="!"]
!===
.2+h![.rotate]#`mcounteren.TM`# .2+h! [.rotate]#`scounteren.TM`# .2+h! [.rotate]#`scounteren.TM`#
4+^.>h! xref:#udb-csr-time[`time`] behavior
.^h! S-mode .^h! U-mode .^h! VS-mode .^h! VU-mode

! 0 ! - ! - ! `Illegal Instruction` ! `Illegal Instruction` ! `Illegal Instruction` ! `Illegal Instruction`
! 1 ! 0 ! 0 ! read-only ! `Illegal Instruction` ! `Illegal Instruction` ! `Illegal Instruction`
! 1 ! 1 ! 0 ! read-only ! read-only ! `Illegal Instruction` ! `Illegal Instruction`
! 1 ! 0 ! 1 ! read-only ! `Illegal Instruction` ! read-only ! `Illegal Instruction`
! 1 ! 1 ! 1 ! read-only ! read-only ! read-only ! read-only
!===
--

== Software read

This CSR may return a value that is different from what is stored in hardware.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
if pass:[(]!TIME_CSR_IMPLEMENTED) {
  xref:#udb-function-unimplemented_csr[unimplemented_csr]pass:[(]$encoding);
}
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::S) {
  if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::U) {
  if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-misa[misa]].xref:#udb-csrfield-misa-S[S] == 1'b1) {
    if pass:[(](CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] & CSR[xref:#udb-csr-scounteren[scounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-scounteren-TM[TM]) == 1'b0) {
      xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
    }
  } else if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::VS) {
  if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-hcounteren[hcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-hcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b0 && CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b1) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::VirtualInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  } else if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]) == PrivilegeMode::VU) {
  if pass:[(]((CSR[xref:#udb-csr-hcounteren[hcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-hcounteren-TM[TM] & CSR[xref:#udb-csr-scounteren[scounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-scounteren-TM[TM]) == 1'b0) && (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b1)) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::VirtualInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  } else if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-mcounteren[mcounteren]].xref:#udb-csrfield-mcounteren-TM[TM] == 1'b0) {
    xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
  }
}
return xref:#udb-function-read_mtime[read_mtime]pass:[(]);

[#udb:doc:inst:c_fld]
= c.fld

Synopsis::
Load double-precision

Mnemonic::
----
c.fld fd, imm(xs1)
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":2,"name": 0x0,"type":2},{"bits":3,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":2,"name": "imm[7:6]","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "imm[5:3]","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Loads a double precision floating-point value from memory into register fd.
It computes an effective address by adding the zero-extended offset, scaled by 8,
to the base address in register xs1.
It expands to xref:#udb-insn-fld[`fld`] `fd, offset(xs1)`.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<8> imm = {$encoding[6:5], $encoding[12:10], 3'd0};
Bits<3> fd = $encoding[4:2];
Bits<3> xs1 = $encoding[9:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-implemented?[implemented?]pass:[(]ExtensionName::C) && MISA_CSR_IMPLEMENTED && (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-misa[misa]].xref:#udb-csrfield-misa-C[C] == 1'b0)) {
  xref:#udb-function-reserved_instruction[reserved_instruction]pass:[(]$encoding);
}
XReg virtual_address = X[xref:#udb-function-creg2reg[creg2reg]pass:[(]xs1)] pass:[+] imm;
F[xref:#udb-function-creg2reg[creg2reg]pass:[(]fd)] = xref:#udb-function-sext[sext]pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-read_memory[read_memory]pass:[(]64, virtual_address, $encoding), 64);
----


Included in::
--
xref:#udb-insn-c_fld[`c.fld`] is unconditionally defined by the following:

|===
| Extension | Version


|===

--

[#udb:doc:inst:c_fldsp]
= c.fldsp

Synopsis::
Load doubleword into floating-point register from stack

Mnemonic::
----
c.fldsp fd, imm(sp)
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":2,"name": 0x2,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm[4:3|8:6]","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":1,"name": "imm[5]","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Loads a double-precision floating-point value from memory into floating-point register fd.
It computes its effective address by adding the zero-extended offset, scaled by 8,
to the stack pointer, x2.
It expands to xref:#udb-insn-fld[`fld`] `fd, offset(x2)`.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<9> imm = {$encoding[4:2], $encoding[12], $encoding[6:5], 3'd0};
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-implemented?[implemented?]pass:[(]ExtensionName::C) && MISA_CSR_IMPLEMENTED && (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-misa[misa]].xref:#udb-csrfield-misa-C[C] == 1'b0)) {
  xref:#udb-function-reserved_instruction[reserved_instruction]pass:[(]$encoding);
}
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-implemented?[implemented?]pass:[(]ExtensionName::D) && MISA_CSR_IMPLEMENTED && (CSR[xref:#udb-csr-misa[misa]].xref:#udb-csrfield-misa-D[D] == 1'b0)) {
  xref:#udb-function-reserved_instruction[reserved_instruction]pass:[(]$encoding);
}
XReg virtual_address = X[2] pass:[+] imm;
F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-read_memory[read_memory]pass:[(]64, virtual_address, $encoding);
----


Included in::
--
xref:#udb-insn-c_fldsp[`c.fldsp`] is unconditionally defined by the following:

| Extension | Version

[#udb:doc:inst:c_flwsp]
= c.flwsp

Synopsis::
Load word into floating-point register from stack

Mnemonic::
----
c.flwsp fd, imm(sp)
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":2,"name": 0x2,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm[4:2|7:6]","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":1,"name": "imm[5]","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x3,"type":2}]}
....

XReg virtual_address = jump_table_base pass:[+] index pass:[`]* (xref:#udb-function-xlen[xlen]pass:[(]) / 8);

if pass:[(]CSR[xref:#udb-csr-misa[misa]].xref:#udb-csrfield-misa-S[S] == 1) {
  result = xref:#udb-function-translate[translate]pass:[(]virtual_address, MemoryOperation::Fetch, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else {

xref:#udb-function-access_check[access_check]pass:[(]result.paddr, xref:#udb-function-xlen[xlen]pass:[(]), $pc, MemoryOperation::Fetch, ExceptionCode::InstructionAccessFault, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]));

[#udb:doc:inst:csrrc]
= csrrc

Synopsis::
Atomic Read and Clear Bits in CSR

Mnemonic::
----
csrrc xd, csr, xs1
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x73,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x3,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "csr","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The CSRRC (Atomic Read and Clear Bits in CSR) instruction reads the value of the CSR, zero-extends
the value to XLEN bits, and writes it to integer register `xd`. The initial value in integer register `xs1` is
treated as a bit mask that specifies bit positions to be cleared in the CSR. Any bit that is high in `xs1` will
cause the corresponding bit to be cleared in the CSR, if that CSR bit is writable.

For CSRRC, if `xs1=x0`, then the instruction will not write to the CSR at all, and so shall
not cause any of the side effects that might otherwise occur on a CSR write, nor raise illegal-
instruction exceptions on accesses to read-only CSRs. CSRRC always reads the addressed CSR and
cause any read side effects regardless of `xs1` and `xd` fields.
Note that if `xs1` specifies a register other than `x0`, and that register holds a zero value,
the instruction will not action any attendant per-field side effects, but will action any
side effects caused by writing to the entire CSR.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<12> csr = $encoding[31:20];
Bits<5> xs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Csr csr_handle = xref:#udb-function-direct_csr_lookup[direct_csr_lookup]pass:[(]csr);
Boolean will_write = xs1 != 0;
if pass:[(]csr_handle.valid == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-unimplemented_csr[unimplemented_csr]pass:[(]$encoding);
} else if pass:[(]!xref:#udb-function-compatible_mode?[compatible_mode?]pass:[(]csr_handle.mode, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]))) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(]will_write && csr_handle.writable == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
}
XReg initial_csr_value = xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_read[csr_sw_read]pass:[(]csr_handle);
if pass:[(]xs1 != 0) {
  XReg mask = X[xs1];
  xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_write[csr_sw_write]pass:[(]csr_handle, initial_csr_value & ~mask);
}
X[xd] = initial_csr_value;
----

[#udb:doc:inst:csrrci]
= csrrci

Synopsis::
Atomic Read and Clear Bits in CSR with Immediate

Mnemonic::
----
csrrci xd, csr, imm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x73,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x7,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "csr","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The CSRRCI variant is similar to CSRRC, except this updates the CSR using an XLEN-bit value obtained
by zero-extending a 5-bit unsigned immediate (imm[4:0]) field encoded in the `xs1` field instead of a
value from an integer register. For CSRRCI, if the `imm[4:0]` field is zero, then this instruction
will not write to the CSR, and shall not cause any of the side effects that might otherwise occur on
a CSR write, nor raise illegal-instruction exceptions on accesses to read-only CSRs. The CSRRCI will
always read the CSR and cause any read side effects regardless of `xd` and `xs1` fields.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<12> csr = $encoding[31:20];
Bits<5> imm = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Boolean will_write = imm != 0;
Csr csr_handle = xref:#udb-function-direct_csr_lookup[direct_csr_lookup]pass:[(]csr);
if pass:[(]csr_handle.valid == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-unimplemented_csr[unimplemented_csr]pass:[(]$encoding);
} else if pass:[(]!xref:#udb-function-compatible_mode?[compatible_mode?]pass:[(]csr_handle.mode, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]))) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(]will_write && csr_handle.writable == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
}
XReg initial_csr_value = xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_read[csr_sw_read]pass:[(]csr_handle);
if pass:[(]will_write) {
  XReg mask = imm;
  xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_write[csr_sw_write]pass:[(]csr_handle, initial_csr_value & ~mask);
}
X[xd] = initial_csr_value;
----

{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x73,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x2,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "csr","type":4}]}
....

Description::
Atomically read and set bits in a CSR.

Reads the value of the CSR, zero-extends the value to `XLEN` bits,
and writes it to integer register `xd`. The initial value in integer
register `xs1` is treated as a bit mask that specifies bit positions
to be set in the CSR. Any bit that is high in `xs1` will cause the
corresponding bit to be set in the CSR, if that CSR bit is writable.
Other bits in the CSR are not explicitly written.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]

Bits<5> xs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Boolean will_write = xs1 != 0;
Csr csr_handle = xref:#udb-function-direct_csr_lookup[direct_csr_lookup]pass:[(]csr);
if pass:[(]csr_handle.valid == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-unimplemented_csr[unimplemented_csr]pass:[(]$encoding);
} else if pass:[(]!xref:#udb-function-compatible_mode?[compatible_mode?]pass:[(]csr_handle.mode, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]))) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(]will_write && csr_handle.writable == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
}
XReg initial_csr_value = xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_read[csr_sw_read]pass:[(]csr_handle);
if pass:[(]will_write) {
  XReg mask = X[xs1];
  xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_write[csr_sw_write]pass:[(]csr_handle, initial_csr_value | mask);
}
X[xd] = initial_csr_value;
----

[#udb:doc:inst:csrrsi]
= csrrsi

Synopsis::
Atomic Read and Set Bits in CSR with Immediate

Mnemonic::
----
csrrsi xd, csr, imm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x73,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x6,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "csr","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The CSRRSI variant is similar to CSRRS, except this updates the CSR using an XLEN-bit value obtained
by zero-extending a 5-bit unsigned immediate (imm[4:0]) field encoded in the `xs1` field instead of a
value from an integer register. For CSRRSI, if the `imm[4:0]` field is zero, then this instruction
will not write to the CSR, and shall not cause any of the side effects that might otherwise occur on
a CSR write, nor raise illegal-instruction exceptions on accesses to read-only CSRs. The CSRRSI will
always read the CSR and cause any read side effects regardless of `xd` and `xs1` fields.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<12> csr = $encoding[31:20];
Bits<5> imm = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----

Csr csr_handle = xref:#udb-function-direct_csr_lookup[direct_csr_lookup]pass:[(]csr);
if pass:[(]csr_handle.valid == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-unimplemented_csr[unimplemented_csr]pass:[(]$encoding);
} else if pass:[(]!xref:#udb-function-compatible_mode?[compatible_mode?]pass:[(]csr_handle.mode, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]))) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(]will_write && csr_handle.writable == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
}
XReg initial_csr_value = xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_read[csr_sw_read]pass:[(]csr_handle);
if pass:[(]will_write) {
  XReg mask = imm;
  xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_write[csr_sw_write]pass:[(]csr_handle, initial_csr_value | mask);
}
X[xd] = initial_csr_value;
----

[#udb:doc:inst:csrrw]
= csrrw

Synopsis::
Atomic Read/Write CSR

Mnemonic::
----
csrrw xd, csr, xs1
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x73,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "csr","type":4}]}
....

Description::
Atomically swap values in the CSRs and integer registers.

Read the old value of the CSR, zero-extends the value to `XLEN` bits,
and then write it to integer register xd.
The initial value in xs1 is written to the CSR.
If `xd=x0`, then the instruction shall not read the CSR and shall not
cause any of the side effects that might occur on a CSR read.

if pass:[(]csr_handle.valid == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-unimplemented_csr[unimplemented_csr]pass:[(]$encoding);
} else if pass:[(]!xref:#udb-function-compatible_mode?[compatible_mode?]pass:[(]csr_handle.mode, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]))) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
} else if pass:[(]csr_handle.writable == false) {
  xref:#udb-function-raise[raise]pass:[(]ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruction, xref:#udb-function-mode[mode]pass:[(]), $encoding);
}
if pass:[(]xd != 0) {
  X[xd] = xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_read[csr_sw_read]pass:[(]csr_handle);
}
xref:#udb-function-csr_sw_write[csr_sw_write]pass:[(]csr_handle, initial_value);
----

[#udb:doc:inst:csrrwi]
= csrrwi

Synopsis::
Atomic Read/Write CSR Immediate

Mnemonic::
----
csrrwi xd, csr, imm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x73,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x5,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "csr","type":4}]}
....

Description::
Atomically write CSR using a 5-bit immediate, and load the previous value into 'xd'.

Read the old value of the CSR, zero-extends the value to `XLEN` bits,
and then write it to integer register xd.
The 5-bit uimm field is zero-extended and written to the CSR.
If `xd=x0`, then the instruction shall not read the CSR and shall not
cause any of the side effects that might occur on a CSR read.

[#udb:doc:inst:fclass_d]
= fclass.d

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Classify Double-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fclass.d xd, fs1
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0xe20,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fclass_d[`fclass.d`] instruction is defined analogously to its single-precision counterpart, but operates on double-precision
operands. It examines the value in floating-point register `fs1` and writes to integer register `xd` a 10-bit mask that
indicates the class of the floating point number.

The format of the mask is described in the table below. The corresponding bit in `xd` will be set if the property
is true and clear otherwise. All other bits in `xd` are cleared. Note that exactly one bit in `xd` will be set.

.Format of result of `fclass` instruction.
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="^,<",options="header",]
|===
|_xd_ bit |Meaning
|0 |_fs1_ is latexmath:[$-\infty$].
|1 |_fs1_ is a negative normal number.
|2 |_fs1_ is a negative subnormal number.
|3 |_fs1_ is latexmath:[$-0$].
|4 |_fs1_ is latexmath:[$+0$].
|5 |_fs1_ is a positive subnormal number.
|6 |_fs1_ is a positive normal number.
|7 |_fs1_ is latexmath:[$+\infty$].
|8 |_fs1_ is a signaling NaN.
|9 |_fs1_ is a quiet NaN.
|===

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----

----

Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value = F[fs1][31:0];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-implemented?[implemented?]pass:[(]ExtensionName::D)) {
  if pass:[(]F[fs1][63:32] != 32'hffffffff) {
    sp_value = SP_CANONICAL_NAN;
  }
}
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_neg_inf?[is_sp_neg_inf?]pass:[(]sp_value)) {
  X[xd] = 1 << 0;
} else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_neg_norm?[is_sp_neg_norm?]pass:[(]sp_value)) {
  X[xd] = 1 pass:[`]<< 1;

Description::
The single-precision to double-precision conversion instruction, xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_d_s[`fcvt.d.s`] is encoded in the OP-FP
major opcode space and both the source and destination are floating-point registers. The `xs2` field
encodes the datatype of the source, and the `fmt` field encodes the datatype of the destination.
xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_d_s[`fcvt.d.s`] will never round.

[#udb:doc:inst:fcvt_h_s]
= fcvt.h.s

Synopsis::
Convert half-precision float to a single-precision float

Mnemonic::
----
fcvt.h.s fd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0x440,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Converts a half-precision number in floating-point register _fs1_ into a single-precision floating-point number in
floating-point register _fd_.

xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_h_s[`fcvt.h.s`] rounds according to the _rm_ field.

All floating-point conversion instructions set the Inexact exception flag if the rounded
result differs from the operand value and the Invalid exception flag is not set.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<16> hp_value = F[fs1][15:0];
Bits<1> sign = hp_value[15];
Bits<5> exp = hp_value[14:10];
Bits<10> frac = hp_value[9:0];
if pass:[(]exp == 0x1F) {
  if pass:[(]frac != 0) {
    if pass:[(](hp_value & 0x0200) != 0) {
      xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
    }
    F[fd] = HP_CANONICAL_NAN;
  } else {
    F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-packToF32UI[packToF32UI]pass:[(]sign, 0xFF, 0);
  }
} else {
  if pass:[(]exp != 0) {
    if pass:[(]frac != 0) {
      F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-packToF32UI[packToF32UI]pass:[(]sign, 0, 0);
    } else {
      Bits<6> norm_exp;
      (norm_exp, frac = xref:#udb-function-softfloat_normSubnormalF16Sig[softfloat_normSubnormalF16Sig]pass:[(]frac));
      exp = norm_exp - 1;
      F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-packToF32UI[packToF32UI]pass:[(]sign, exp pass:[+] 0x70, frac << 13);
    }
  } else {
    F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-packToF32UI[packToF32UI]pass:[(]sign, exp pass:[+] 0x70, frac << 13);
  }
}
xref:#udb-function-mark_f_state_dirty[mark_f_state_dirty]pass:[(]);
----

[#udb:doc:inst:fcvt_s_d]
= fcvt.s.d

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Convert Double-Precision to Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fcvt.s.d fd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0x401,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_s_d[`fcvt.s.d`] instruction converts a double-precision floating-point number to a single-precision floating-point
number. This is encoded in the OP-FP major opcode space and both the source and destination are floating-point
registers. The `xs2` field encodes the datatype of the source, and the `fmt` field encodes the datatype of the
destination. xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_s_d[`fcvt.s.d`] rounds according to the `rm` field.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----

----


Included in::
--
xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_s_d[`fcvt.s.d`] is unconditionally defined by the following:

|===
| Extension | Version


|===

--

:leveloffset: -2

<<<
:leveloffset: +2


[#udb:doc:inst:fcvt_s_h]
= fcvt.s.h

Synopsis::
Convert single-precision float to a half-precision float

Mnemonic::
----
fcvt.s.h fd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0x402,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Converts a single-precision number in floating-point register _fs1_ into a half-precision floating-point number in
floating-point register _fd_.

xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_s_h[`fcvt.s.h`] will never round, and so the 'rm' field is effectively ignored.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<1> sign = sp_value[31];
Bits<8> exp = sp_value[30:23];
Bits<23> frac = sp_value[22:0];
if pass:[(]exp == 0xFF) {
  if pass:[(]frac != 0) {
    if pass:[(](sp_value & 0x00400000) != 0) {
      xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
    }
    F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-nan_box[nan_box]pass:[(]16, FLEN, HP_CANONICAL_NAN);
  } else {
    F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-nan_box[nan_box]pass:[(]16, FLEN, xref:#udb-function-packToF16UI[packToF16UI]pass:[(]sign, 0x1F, 0));
  }
} else {
  Bits<16> frac16 = (frac >> 9) | ((frac & 0x1ff) != 0 ? 1 : 0);
  if pass:[(](exp | frac16) == 0) {
    F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-nan_box[nan_box]pass:[(]16, FLEN, xref:#udb-function-packToF16UI[packToF16UI]pass:[(]sign, 0, 0));
  } else {
    xref:#udb-function-assert[assert]pass:[(]false, "TODO: implement roundPackToF16");
  }
}
xref:#udb-function-mark_f_state_dirty[mark_f_state_dirty]pass:[(]);
----


Included in::
--
xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_s_h[`fcvt.s.h`] is unconditionally defined by the following:

|===
| Extension | Version


|===

--

[#udb:doc:inst:fcvt_w_s]
= fcvt.w.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Convert Single-Precision to Word

Mnemonic::
----
fcvt.w.s xd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0xc00,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_w_s[`fcvt.w.s`] instruction converts a floating-point number in floating-point register `fs1` to a
signed 32-bit integer in integer register `xd`. For XLEN &gt;32, xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_w_s[`fcvt.w.s`] sign-extends the 32-bit
result to the destination register width.

If the rounded result is not representable as a 32-bit signed integer, it is clipped to the
nearest value and the invalid flag is set.

The range of valid inputs and behavior for invalid inputs are:

[separator="!"]
!===
! ! Value

h! Minimum valid input (after rounding) ! `-2^31`
h! Maximum valid input (after rounding) ! `2^31 - 1`
h! Output for out-of-range negative input ! `-2^31`
h! Output for `−∞` ! `-2^31`
h! Output for out-of-range positive input ! `2^31 - 1`
h! Output for `+∞` for `NaN` ! `2^31 - 1`
!===

All floating-point to integer and integer to floating-point conversion instructions round
according to the `rm` field.

A floating-point register can be initialized to floating-point positive zero using
`fcvt.s.w xd, x0`, which will never set any exception flags.

All floating-point conversion instructions set the Inexact exception flag if the rounded
result differs from the operand value and the Invalid exception flag is not set.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
RoundingMode rounding_mode = xref:#udb-function-rm_to_mode[rm_to_mode]pass:[(]rm, $encoding);
X[xd] = $signed+++(+++xref:#udb-function-f32_to_i32[f32_to_i32]pass:[(]F[fs1], rounding_mode));
----

Description::
Converts a floating-point number in floating-point register `fs1` to an unsigned 32-bit integer
in integer register `xd`. For XLEN &gt;32, xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_wu_s[`fcvt.wu.s`] sign-extends the 32-bit result to the
destination register width.

If the rounded result is not representable as a 32-bit unsigned integer, it is clipped to the
nearest value and the invalid flag is set.

The range of valid inputs and behavior for invalid inputs are:

[separator="!"]
!===
! ! Value

h! Minimum valid input (after rounding) ! `0`
h! Maximum valid input (after rounding) ! `2^32 - 1`
h! Output for out-of-range negative input ! `0`
h! Output for `−∞` ! `0`
h! Output for out-of-range positive input ! `2^32 - 1`
h! Output for `+∞` for `NaN` ! `2^32 - 1`
!===

All floating-point to integer and integer to floating-point conversion instructions round
according to the `rm` field.

A floating-point register can be initialized to floating-point positive zero using
`fcvt.s.w xd, x0`, which will never set any exception flags.

All floating-point conversion instructions set the Inexact exception flag if the rounded
result differs from the operand value and the Invalid exception flag is not set.

[#udb:doc:inst:fcvtmod_w_d]
= fcvtmod.w.d

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Convert Double-Precision to Word with Modulo

Mnemonic::
----
fcvtmod.w.d xd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm != {0,2,3,4,5,6,7}","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0xc28,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fcvtmod_w_d[`fcvtmod.w.d`] instruction always rounds towards zero. Bits 31:0 are taken from the rounded, unbounded
two's complement result, then sign-extended to XLEN bits and written to integer register `xd`. ±∞ and
NaN are converted to zero.

Floating-point exception flags are raised the same as they would be for xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_w_d[`fcvt.w.d`] with the same input
operand.

This instruction is only provided if the D extension is implemented. It is encoded like xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_w_d[`fcvt.w.d`], but
with the `xs2` field set to 8 and the `rm` field set to 1 (RTZ). Other `rm` values are reserved.

Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----

----

[#udb:doc:inst:fdiv_q]
= fdiv.q

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Divide Quad-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fdiv.q fd, fs1, fs2, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0xf,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fdiv_q[`fdiv.q`] performs the quad-precision floating-point division of `fs1` by `fs2` and writes
the result to floating-point register `fd`.`
The rounding mode is specified by the value in the floating-point Control and Status register (FCSR)
or by the value in the `rm` field of the instruction.

The operation is performed according to the IEEE 754-2008 standard for quad-precision floating-point arithmetic.

The instruction sets the floating-point exception flags according to the result of the operation.

= feq.q

feq.q xd, fs1, fs2

it only sets the invalid operation exception flag if either input is a signaling _NaN_.

[#udb:doc:inst:feq_s]
= feq.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Equal Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
feq.s xd, fs1, fs2
----

[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x2,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x50,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-feq_s[`feq.s`] instruction writes 1 to `xd` if `fs1` and `fs2` are equal, and 0 otherwise.
If either operand is NaN, the result is 0 (not equal). If either operand is a signaling NaN,
the invalid flag is set. Positive zero is considered equal to negative zero.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]

Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value_a = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<32> sp_value_b = F[fs2][31:0];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
  if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_signaling_nan?[is_sp_signaling_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_signaling_nan?[is_sp_signaling_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
    xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
  }
  X[xd] = 0;
} else {
  X[xd] = ((sp_value_a == sp_value_b) || ((sp_value_a | sp_value_b)[30:0] == 0)) ? 1 : 0;
}
----

[#udb:doc:inst:fle_s]
= fle.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Less Than or Equal Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fle.s xd, fs1, fs2
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x0,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x50,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fle_s[`fle.s`] instruction writes 1 to `xd` if `fs1` is less than or equal to `fs2`, and 0 otherwise.
If either operand is NaN, the result is 0 (not equal). If either operand is a NaN (signaling or quiet),
the invalid flag is set. Positive zero and negative zero are considered equal.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value_a = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<32> sp_value_b = F[fs2][31:0];
Bits<1> sign_a = sp_value_a[31];
Bits<1> sign_b = sp_value_b[31];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
  xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
  X[xd] = 0;
} else {
  if pass:[(]sign_a != sign_b) {
    X[xd] = ((sign_a == 1) || ((sp_value_a[30:0] | sp_value_b[30:0]) == 0)) ? 1 : 0;
  } else {
    X[xd] = ((sp_value_a == sp_value_b) || (sign_a ^ ((sp_value_a < sp_value_b) ? 1 : 0)) == 1) ? 1 : 0;
  }
}
----

= fleq.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Less Than or Equal Quiet Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fleq.s xd, fs1, fs2
----

[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x4,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x50,"type":2}]}

The xref:#udb-insn-fleq_s[`fleq.s`] instruction is defined like the xref:#udb-insn-fle_s[`fle.s`] instruction, except that quiet NaN inputs do
not cause the invalid operation exception flag to be set.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value_a = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<32> sp_value_b = F[fs2][31:0];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
  if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_signaling_nan?[is_sp_signaling_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_signaling_nan?[is_sp_signaling_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
    xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
  }
  X[xd] = 0;
} else {
  Boolean sign_a = sp_value_a[31] == 1;
  Boolean sign_b = sp_value_b[31] == 1;
  Boolean a_eq_b = (sp_value_a == sp_value_b) || ((sp_value_a | sp_value_b)[30:0] == 0);
  Boolean a_lt_b;
  if pass:[(]sign_a != sign_b) {
    a_lt_b = sign_a && ((sp_value_a[30:0] | sp_value_b[30:0]) != 0);
  } else {
    a_lt_b = (sp_value_a != sp_value_b) && (sign_a != (sp_value_a < sp_value_b));
  }
  X[xd] = (a_lt_b || a_eq_b) ? 1 : 0;
}
----

[#udb:doc:inst:flh]
= flh

Synopsis::
Half-precision floating-point load

Mnemonic::
----
flh fd, imm(xs1)
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x7,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": "imm","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-flh[`flh`] instruction loads a half-precision floating-point value from memory at address _xs1_ + _imm_ into floating-point register _xd_.

xref:#udb-insn-flh[`flh`] does not modify the bits being transferred; in particular, the payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved.

xref:#udb-insn-flh[`flh`] is only guaranteed to execute atomically if the effective address is naturally aligned.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
signed Bits<12> imm = sext($encoding[31:20], 12);
Bits<5> xs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
XReg virtual_address = X[xs1] pass:[+] $signed+++(+++imm);

xref:#udb-insn-flq[`flq`] is only guaranteed to execute atomically if the effective address is naturally aligned XLEN=128.

_NaNs_ are preserved.

[#udb:doc:inst:flt_q]
= flt.q

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Less Than Quad-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
flt.q xd, fs1, fs2
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-flt_q[`flt.q`] performs the specified comparison between floating-point registers `fs1` and `fs2`,
and writes 1 to integer register `xd` if the conditon hold, and 0 otherwise.


xref:#udb-insn-flt_q[`flt.q`] performs what the IEEE 754-2008 standard refers to as signaling comparisons: that is,
they set the invalid operation exception flag if either input is _NaN_.
The result is 0 if either operand is _NaN_.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]

:leveloffset: -2

<<<
:leveloffset: +2


[#udb:doc:inst:flt_s]
= flt.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Less Than Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
flt.s xd, fs1, fs2
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x50,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-flt_s[`flt.s`] instruction writes 1 to `xd` if `fs1` is less than `fs2`, and 0 otherwise.
If either operand is NaN, the result is 0 (not equal). If either operand is a NaN
(signaling or quiet), the invalid flag is set.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value_a = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<32> sp_value_b = F[fs2][31:0];
Bits<1> sign_a = sp_value_a[31];
Bits<1> sign_b = sp_value_b[31];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
  xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
  X[xd] = 0;
} else {
  if pass:[(]sign_a != sign_b) {
    X[xd] = ((sign_a == 1) && ((sp_value_a[30:0] | sp_value_b[30:0]) != 0)) ? 1 : 0;
  } else {
    X[xd] = ((sp_value_a != sp_value_b) && ((sign_a ^ ((sp_value_a < sp_value_b) ? 1 : 0)) != 0)) ? 1 : 0;
  }
}
----

Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value_a = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<32> sp_value_b = F[fs2][31:0];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {

    xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
  }
  X[xd] = 0;
} else {
  Boolean sign_a = sp_value_a[31] == 1;
  Boolean sign_b = sp_value_b[31] == 1;
  Boolean a_lt_b = (sign_a != sign_b) ? (sign_a && ((sp_value_a[30:0] | sp_value_b[30:0]) != 0)) : ((sp_value_a != sp_value_b) && (sign_a != (sp_value_a < sp_value_b)));

:leveloffset: +2


[#udb:doc:inst:fmadd_q]
= fmadd.q

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Multiply-Add Quad-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fmadd.q fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x43,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":2,"name": 0x3,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs3","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fmadd_q[`fmadd.q`] instruction performs a floating-point multiply-add operation on the values in registers `fs1`, `fs2`, and `fs3`.
It computes the result as `(fs1 * fs2) + fs3` and writes the result to the destination register `fd`.

The fused multiply-add instructions must set the invalid operation exception flag when the
multiplicands are latexmath:[$\infty$] and zero, even when the addend is a quiet _NaN_.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs3 = $encoding[31:27];
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----

----


Included in::
--
xref:#udb-insn-fmadd_q[`fmadd.q`] is unconditionally defined by the following:

|===
| Extension | Version


|===

--

:leveloffset: -2

<<<
:leveloffset: +2


[#udb:doc:inst:fmadd_s]
= fmadd.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Multiply-Add Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fmadd.s fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x43,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":2,"name": 0x0,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs3","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fmadd_s[`fmadd.s`] multiplies the values in `fs1` and `fs2`, adds the value in `fs3`, and writes the final result to `fd`.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs3 = $encoding[31:27];
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
RoundingMode mode = xref:#udb-function-rm_to_mode[rm_to_mode]pass:[(]rm, $encoding);
F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-f32_muladd[f32_muladd]pass:[(]F[fs1], F[fs2], F[fs3], F32MulAddOp::Softfloat_mulAdd_addC, mode);
xref:#udb-function-mark_f_state_dirty[mark_f_state_dirty]pass:[(]);
----

[#udb:doc:inst:fmax_d]
= fmax.d

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Maximum-Number Double-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fmax.d fd, fs1, fs2
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x15,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fmax_d[`fmax.d`] instruction writes larger of `fs1` and `fs2` to `fd`. For the purposes of this instruction,
the value `-0.0` is considered to be less than the value `+0.0`. If both inputs are NaNs, the result is
the canonical NaN. If only one operand is a NaN, the result is the non-NaN operand. Signaling NaN inputs
set the invalid operation exception flag, even when the result is not NaN.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----

----

| Extension | Version


|===

[#udb:doc:inst:fmax_s]
= fmax.s

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Maximum-Number Single-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fmax.s fd, fs1, fs2
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x14,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fmax_s[`fmax.s`] instruction writes larger of `fs1` and `fs2` to `fd`. For the purposes of this instruction,
the value `-0.0` is considered to be less than the value `+0.0`. If both inputs are NaNs, the result is
the canonical NaN. If only one operand is a NaN, the result is the non-NaN operand. Signaling NaN inputs
set the invalid operation exception flag, even when the result is not NaN.

[NOTE]
====
Note that in version 2.2 of the F extension, the xref:#udb-insn-fmin_s[`fmin.s`] and xref:#udb-insn-fmax_s[`fmax.s`] instructions were amended to implement
the proposed _IEEE 754-201x_ `minimumNumber` and `maximumNumber` operations, rather than the _IEEE 754-2008_
_minNum_ and _maxNum_ operations. These operations differ in their handling of signaling NaNs.
====



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
Bits<32> sp_value_a = F[fs1][31:0];
Bits<32> sp_value_b = F[fs2][31:0];
if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
  if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_signaling_nan?[is_sp_signaling_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) || xref:#udb-function-is_sp_signaling_nan?[is_sp_signaling_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
    xref:#udb-function-set_fp_flag[set_fp_flag]pass:[(]FpFlag::NV);
  }
  if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a) && xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_b)) {
    F[fd] = SP_CANONICAL_NAN;
  } else if pass:[(]xref:#udb-function-is_sp_nan?[is_sp_nan?]pass:[(]sp_value_a)) {
    F[fd] = sp_value_b;
  } else {
    F[fd] = sp_value_a;
  }
} else {
  Boolean sign_a = sp_value_a[31] == 1;
  Boolean sign_b = sp_value_b[31] == 1;
  Boolean a_lt_b;
  if pass:[(]sign_a != sign_b) {
    a_lt_b = sign_a;
  } else {
    a_lt_b = (sp_value_a != sp_value_b) && (sign_a != (sp_value_a < sp_value_b));
  }
  F[fd] = a_lt_b ? sp_value_b : sp_value_a;
}
xref:#udb-function-mark_f_state_dirty[mark_f_state_dirty]pass:[(]);
----

= fminm.q

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Minimum-Number NaN Quad-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fminm.q fd, fs1, fs2
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]

{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x2,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": 0x17,"type":2}]}

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fminm_q[`fminm.q`] instruction, defined like xref:#udb-insn-fmin_q[`fmin.q`], writes the smaller/minimum of `fs1` and `fs2` to `fd`.
The value `-0.0` is considered to be less than the value `+0.0`.
If both inputs are _NaN_s, the result is the canonical _NaN_.
If either input is _NaN_, the result is the canonical _NaN_.
Signaling _NaN_ inputs set the invalid operation exception flag, even when the result is not _NaN_.

[#udb:doc:inst:fmv_h_x]
= fmv.h.x

Synopsis::
Half-precision floating-point move from integer

Mnemonic::
----
fmv.h.x fd, xs1
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x0,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0xf40,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Moves the half-precision value encoded in IEEE 754-2008 standard encoding
from the lower 16 bits of integer register `xs1` to the floating-point
register `fd`. The bits are not modified in the transfer, and in particular,
the payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]

Bits<5> xs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];

Bits<16> hp_value = X[xs1][15:0];
F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-nan_box[nan_box]pass:[(]16, FLEN, hp_value);
xref:#udb-function-mark_f_state_dirty[mark_f_state_dirty]pass:[(]);

[#udb:doc:inst:fmv_x_h]
= fmv.x.h

Synopsis::
Move half-precision value from floating-point to integer register

Mnemonic::
----
fmv.x.h xd, fs1
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x0,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0xe40,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Moves the half-precision value in floating-point register fs1 represented in IEEE 754-2008
encoding to the lower 16 bits of integer register xd.

The bits are not modified in the transfer, and in particular, the payloads of non-canonical
NaNs are preserved.

The highest XLEN-16 bits of the destination register are filled with copies of the
floating-point number's sign bit.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]

[#udb:doc:inst:fmv_x_w]
= fmv.x.w

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Move Single-Precision Word to Integer Register

Mnemonic::
----
fmv.x.w xd, fs1
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x0,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0xe00,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fmv_x_w[`fmv.x.w`] instruction moves the single-precision value in floating-point register `fs1`` represented in `IEEE 754-2008`
encoding to the lower 32 bits of integer register `xd`. The bits are not modified in the transfer, and in particular, the
payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved. For RV64, the higher 32 bits of the destination register are filled with copies
of the floating-point number's sign bit.

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> xd = $encoding[11:7];
----

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
X[xd] = xref:#udb-function-sext[sext]pass:[(]F[fs1][31:0], 32);
----

[#udb:doc:inst:fround_d]
= fround.d

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Round Double-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fround.d fd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0x424,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fround_d[`fround.d`] instruction rounds the double-precision floating-point number in floating-point register
`fs1` to an integer, according to the rounding mode specified in the instruction's `rm` field. It then writes
that integer, represented as a double-precision floating-point number, to floating-point register `fd`. Zero
and infinite inputs are copied to `fd` unmodified. Signaling NaN inputs cause the invalid operation
exception flag to be set; no other exception flags are set. xref:#udb-insn-fround_d[`fround.d`] is encoded like xref:#udb-insn-fcvt_d_s[`fcvt.d.s`], but with
`xs2` set to 4.

Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];

<<<

[#udb:doc:inst:fround_s]
= fround.s

Synopsis::
Floating-point round single-precision float to integer

Mnemonic::
----
fround.s fd, fs1, rm
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x53,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "fd","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": "rm","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs1","type":4},{"bits":12,"name": 0x404,"type":2}]}
....

Description::
Rounds the single-precision floating-point number in floating-point register _fs1_ to an integer, according to the rounding mode specified in the instruction’s _rm_ field.

It then writes that integer, represented as a single-precision floating-point number, to floating-point register _fd_.

Zero and infinite inputs are copied to _fd_ unmodified.

Signaling NaN inputs cause the invalid operation exception flag to be set; no other exception flags are set. FROUND.S is encoded like FCVT.S.D, but with rs2=4.



Decode Variables::

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
Bits<5> fs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<3> rm = $encoding[14:12];
Bits<5> fd = $encoding[11:7];
----


Operation::
[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
RoundingMode rounding_mode = xref:#udb-function-rm_to_mode[rm_to_mode]pass:[(]rm, $encoding);
F[fd] = xref:#udb-function-round_f32_to_integral[round_f32_to_integral]pass:[(]F[fs1], rounding_mode);
xref:#udb-function-mark_f_state_dirty[mark_f_state_dirty]pass:[(]);
----

[#udb:doc:inst:fsd]
= fsd

Synopsis::
Floating-Point Store Double-Precision

Mnemonic::
----
fsd fs2, imm(xs1)
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x27,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm[4:0]","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x3,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": "imm[11:5]","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fsd[`fsd`] instruction stores a double-precision value from the floating-point registers to memory. It is guaranteed to execute
atomically if the effective address is naturally aligned and XLEN≥64. It doesn't modify the bits being transferred; in particular,
the payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved.

fsgnj.d fd, fs1, fs2

[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....

....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fsgnj_d[`fsgnj.d`] instruction produces a result that takes all bits except the sign bit from `fs1`.
The result's sign bit is taken from `fs2`'s sign bit, and the result is written to the destination
register `fd`. Sign-injection instructions do not set floating-point exception flags, nor do they
canonicalize NaNs.

xref:#udb-insn-fsgnjx_q[`fsgnjx.q`] does not set floating-point exception flags, nor do they canonicalize _NaN_s.

[#udb:doc:inst:fsh]
= fsh

Synopsis::
Half-precision floating-point store

Mnemonic::
----
fsh fs2, imm(xs1)
----

Encoding::
[wavedrom, ,svg,subs='attributes',width="100%"]
....
{"reg":[{"bits":7,"name": 0x27,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "imm[4:0]","type":4},{"bits":3,"name": 0x1,"type":2},{"bits":5,"name": "xs1","type":4},{"bits":5,"name": "fs2","type":4},{"bits":7,"name": "imm[11:5]","type":4}]}
....

Description::
The xref:#udb-insn-fsh[`fsh`] instruction stores a half-precision floating-point value
from register _xd_ to memory at address _xs1_ + _imm_.

xref:#udb-insn-fsh[`fsh`] does not modify the bits being transferred; in particular, the payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved.

xref:#udb-insn-fsh[`fsh`] ignores all but the lower 16 bits in _fs2_.

xref:#udb-insn-fsh[`fsh`] is only guaranteed to execute atomically if the effective address is naturally aligned.

signed Bits<12> imm = sext({$encoding[31:25], $encoding[11:7]}, 12);
Bits<5> xs1 = $encoding[19:15];
Bits<5> fs2 = $encoding[24:20];
----

[source,idl,subs="specialchars,macros"]
----
xref:#udb-function-check_f_ok[check_f_ok]pass:[(]$encoding);
XReg virtual_address = X[xs1] pass:[+] $signed+++(+++imm);
Bits<16> hp_value = F[fs2][15:0];
xref:#udb-function-write_memory[write_memory]pass:[(]16, virtual_address, hp_value, $encoding);
----
